\displaystyle\leq\|F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{2}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}+\|F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{2}X^{k_{3}}({\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet})\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Pi_{2}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}+\|({\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}-{\mathcal{S}})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq O(\sqrt{t/N}).

The first term is zero by the definitions of
\{{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}
. The second term is bounded by
Lemma

4.35
. This completes the proof of
Lemma

6.2
.
∎

Lemma 6.3

(Closeness of
F_{1}^{L,\dagger}
and
F_{1}^{R,\dagger}
)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(\sqrt{t/N})

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{1}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(\sqrt{t/N}).

Proof.

Let
\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{1}^{L})}
denote the projection onto the image of
F_{1}^{L}
. For an arbitrary normalized state
\lvert\psi\rangle
on registers
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}_{1},\mathsf{T}_{1},\mathsf{S}_{2},\mathsf{T}_{2}
in the subspace of
\Pi_{\leq t}
, we can decompose it as

\lvert\psi\rangle=\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{1}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle+(\mathrm{id}-\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{1}^{L})})\lvert\psi\rangle.

We will show the following two bounds

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L,\dagger})\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{1}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})

(35)

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{1}}F^{\dagger,L}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L,\dagger})(\mathrm{id}-\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{1}^{L})})\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})

(36)

which then the first bound follows by the triangle inequality. Notice that
F^{L,\dagger}(\mathrm{id}-\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{1}^{L})})=0
, so
Equation

36
follows from
Lemma

6.2
. Hence, it remains to prove
Equation

35
. Since
\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{1}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle
is in the image of
F_{1}^{L}
, there exists some state
\lvert\phi\rangle
such that
\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{1}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle=F_{1}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle
. Now, we bound
Equation

35
by the triangle inequality as follows:

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L,\dagger})\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{1}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L,\dagger})F_{1}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}-X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\phi\rangle}\|_{2}

(37)

\displaystyle\hskip 68.99895pt+\|{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\phi\rangle}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L,\dagger}F_{1}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}.

(38)

To bound
Equation

37
, we have

\displaystyle\|X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle-X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle-X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}X^{k_{3}}X^{k_{3}}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}

(since
X^{k_{3}}X^{k_{3}}=\mathrm{id}
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}\cdot({\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}-X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}})\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}\cdot({\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}-X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|F^{L,\dagger}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\|({\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}-X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

(since operator norm is submultiplicative)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(\sqrt{t/N}).

(by the fact that
F^{L}
is a contraction and
Lemma

6.1
)

To bound
Equation

38
, we first expand the terms. By
Lemma

3.10
, we can replace
F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}
with
\mathrm{id}
as follows:

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L,\dagger}F_{1}^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}X^{k_{1}}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|({\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}X^{k_{1}}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L,\dagger}F_{1}^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}-\mathrm{id})X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|(\mathrm{id}-F_{1}^{L,\dagger}F_{1}^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}-\mathrm{id})\Pi_{\leq 3t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|(\mathrm{id}-F_{1}^{L,\dagger}F_{1}^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(t/N).

This proves the first bound. The second bound follows symmetrically.
∎

6.3
Putting Things Together

Finally, we use the above lemmas to prove
Lemma

4.37
. We restate
Lemma

4.37
for convenience.

Lemma 6.4

(
Lemma

4.37
, restated)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

•

Forward query:

\|(X^{k_{3}}FX^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})
,

•

Inverse query:

\|(X^{k_{1}}F^{\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})
.

Proof of
Lemma

4.37
.

Forward query.

Recall the definition of
F_{1}
:

\displaystyle F_{1}

\displaystyle=F_{1}^{L}\cdot(\mathrm{id}-F_{1}^{R}\cdot F_{1}^{R,\dagger})+(\mathrm{id}-F_{1}^{L}\cdot F_{1}^{L,\dagger})\cdot F_{1}^{R,\dagger}

\displaystyle=F_{1}^{L}-F_{1}^{L}\cdot F_{1}^{R}\cdot F_{1}^{R,\dagger}+F_{1}^{R,\dagger}-F_{1}^{L}\cdot F_{1}^{L,\dagger}\cdot F_{1}^{R,\dagger}.

Similarly, we expand
X^{k_{3}}FX^{k_{1}}
in the following:

\displaystyle X^{k_{3}}FX^{k_{1}}=

\displaystyle X^{k_{3}}F^{L}\cdot(\mathrm{id}-F^{R}F^{R,\dagger})X^{k_{1}}+X^{k_{3}}(\mathrm{id}-F^{L}F^{L,\dagger})\cdot F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}-X^{k_{3}}F^{L}F^{R}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}+X^{k_{3}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}-X^{k_{3}}F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}-(X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}})(X^{k_{1}}F^{R}X^{k_{3}})(X^{k_{3}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}})

\displaystyle+X^{k_{3}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}-(X^{k_{3}}F_{L}X^{k_{1}})(X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}})(X^{k_{3}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}).

By the triangle inequality, it suffices to bound each of the following terms:

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{3}}FX^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

(39)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|((X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}})(X^{k_{1}}F^{R}X^{k_{3}})(X^{k_{3}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}){\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}F_{1}^{R}F_{1}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

(40)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{3}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

(41)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|((X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}})(X^{k_{1}}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}})(X^{k_{3}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}){\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}F_{1}^{L,\dagger}F_{1}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

(42)

From
Lemma

6.1
, we can bound
Equation

39
by
O(\sqrt{t/N})
. From
Lemma

6.3
, we can bound
Equation

41
by
O(\sqrt{t^{2}/N})
. To bound
Equation

40
, by the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|((X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}})(X^{k_{1}}F^{R}X^{k_{3}})(X^{k_{3}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}){\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}F_{1}^{R}F_{1}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|((X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}})(X^{k_{1}}F^{R}X^{k_{3}}){\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}(X^{k_{3}}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{1}}){\mathcal{S}}}-(X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}})(X^{k_{1}}F^{R}X^{k_{3}}){\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{R,\dagger}})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\hskip 34.5021pt+\|((X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}){\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}(X^{k_{1}}F^{R}X^{k_{3}}){\mathcal{S}}}F_{1}^{R,\dagger}-(X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}){\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{R}}F_{1}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\hskip 68.99895pt+\|({\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}(X^{k_{3}}F^{L}X^{k_{1}}){\mathcal{S}}}F_{1}^{R}F_{1}^{R,\dagger}-{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}{\mathcal{S}}F_{1}^{L}}F_{1}^{R}F_{1}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(\sqrt{t/N})

where we use (i) that operator norm is submultiplicative; (ii) that
F^{L},F^{R}
are contractions; and (iii)
Lemma

6.3
,
Lemma

6.1
, and
Lemma

6.1
, in order, for the colored parts. Similarly,
Equation

42
is at most
O(t/\sqrt{N})
by
Lemma

6.3
,
Lemma

6.3
, and
Lemma

6.1
. Combining the above terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(X^{k_{3}}FX^{k_{1}}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{1})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(\sqrt{t/N}).

This proves the first item of
Lemma

4.37
. The proof of the second item follows by symmetry. This completes the proof of
Lemma

4.37
.
∎

7
Closeness of the Second Oracle: Proving
Lemma

4.38

The high-level proof strategy is similar to that in
Section

6
, where we bound each pair of terms separately. The main distinction is that a query to
FX^{k_{2}}F
involves two calls to
F
, which makes the calculation more intricate. We start by listing some helpful lemmas.

7.1
Closeness of
F_{2}^{L}
and
F_{2}^{R}

Lemma 7.1

(Closeness of
F_{2}^{L}
and
F_{2}^{R}
)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|(F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(\sqrt{t/N})

\displaystyle\|(F^{R}X^{k_{2}}F^{R}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(\sqrt{t/N}).

Proof.

Fix
t\in\mathbb{N}
,
x\in[N],L_{1},L_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t}
, and
R_{1},R_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{D}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t}
. We start by calculating the following states:

\displaystyle\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{ASTK}}:=F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}},

\displaystyle\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{ASTK}}:={\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}.

Computing
\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
.

Expanding the definitions of
{\mathcal{S}}
and
F^{L}
, we have

\displaystyle\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle\xmapsto{{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+3}}}\sum_{\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}

(by
Lemma

4.34
)

\displaystyle\xmapsto{F^{L}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+4}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
z^{\prime}\notin\imaginary(L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})})\end{subarray}}\lvert z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\cup\{(x,z^{\prime})\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}

(by
Equation

1
)

\displaystyle\xmapsto{F^{L}X^{k_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
z^{\prime},y\notin\imaginary(L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}):\\
y\neq z^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\cup\{(x,z^{\prime}),(z^{\prime}\oplus k_{2},y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}},

(43)

where the last line is by
Equation

1
.

Calculating
\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
.

Similarly, expanding the definitions of
{\mathcal{S}}
and
F_{2}^{L}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\notin\imaginary(L_{2})\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup(L_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\})^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}.

(44)

Orthogonality.

Consider distinct
(x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2})
and
(x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2})
. We claim that

•

\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
is orthogonal to
\lvert\psi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle
,

•

\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
is orthogonal to
\lvert\phi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle
,

•

\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
is orthogonal to
\lvert\phi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle
.

They together implies that
\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle-\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
is orthogonal to
\lvert\psi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle-\lvert\phi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle
. Thus, by
Lemma

3.4
, it suffices to maximize the norm over input states of the form
\lvert x\rangle\lvert L_{1}\rangle\lvert R_{1}\rangle\lvert L_{2}\rangle\lvert R_{2}\rangle
. To prove the claim, we define the operator

{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq{\mathcal{D}}\cdot F_{\mathsf{extract}}^{L}\cdot X^{k_{2}}\cdot F_{\mathsf{extract}}^{L}

where the partial isometry
F_{\mathsf{extract}}^{L}
is defined in
Equation

6
. Note that
{\mathcal{I}}
preserves inner product between the states under consideration. To see this, we may compute the states obtained by applying
{\mathcal{I}}
to them:

\displaystyle\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle

(45)

\displaystyle\xmapsto{F_{\mathsf{extract}}^{L}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
z^{\prime},y\notin\imaginary(L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}):\\
y\neq z^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\lvert z^{\prime}\oplus k_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\cup\{(x,z^{\prime})\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}

(by
Equation

6
)

\displaystyle\xmapsto{F_{\mathsf{extract}}^{L}X^{k_{2}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
z^{\prime},y\notin\imaginary(L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}):\\
y\neq z^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\lvert z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime\prime}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}

(by
Equation

6
)

\displaystyle\xmapsto{{\mathcal{D}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
z^{\prime},y\notin\imaginary(L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}):\\
y\neq z^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\lvert z^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime\prime}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}\lvert\mathbf{z}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}},

(46)

where the last line is by
Definition

4.13
and noting that the state in the third line is entirely in the domain of
{\mathcal{D}}
. From the above calculation, it is clear that
{\mathcal{I}}\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
is orthogonal to
{\mathcal{I}}\lvert\psi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle
whenever
(x,L_{1},L_{2},{R}_{1},{R}_{2})\neq(x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},{R^{\prime}}_{1},{R^{\prime}}_{2})
.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle

\displaystyle\xmapsto{{\mathcal{I}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\notin\imaginary(L_{2})\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\,\imaginary(L_{2})\cup\{y\}\end{subarray}}\lvert z_{L,i}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime\prime}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}\lvert\vec{z}_{L,-i}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{L}}}\lvert\vec{z}_{R}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{R}}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}.

(47)

Note that
\vec{z}_{L}
is of length
\imaginary(|L_{2}|)+1
. In the above expression,
i\in[|\imaginary(L_{2})|+1]
is the index such that
y
is the
i
-th largest element in
\imaginary(L_{2})\cup\{y\}
;
z_{L,i}
is the
i
-th coordinate of
\vec{z}_{L}
; and
\vec{z}_{L,-i}
is the vector obtained by removing the
i
-th coordinate of
\vec{z}_{L}
.

By rearranging, we can express it as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\notin\imaginary(L_{2}),\mathbf{k},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}z\in[N],\vec{z}_{L}\in[N]^{|L_{2}|}_{\mathrm{dist}}},\vec{z}_{R}\in[N]^{|R_{2}|}_{\mathrm{dist}}:\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\,\imaginary(L_{2})\cup\{y\}\\
(\mathbf{k},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\vec{z}^{\,(i\leftarrow z)}_{L}},\vec{z}_{R})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\end{subarray}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}z}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime\prime}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\vec{z}_{L}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{L}}}\lvert\vec{z}_{R}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{R}}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}},

(48)

where
\vec{z}^{\,(i\leftarrow z)}_{L}
denotes the vector obtained by inserting
z
into the
i
-th coordinate of
\vec{z}_{L}
and shifting all subsequent coordinates by one.

Likewise,
{\mathcal{I}}\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
is orthogonal to
{\mathcal{I}}\lvert\phi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle
and
{\mathcal{I}}
preserves the inner product between
\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
and
\lvert\phi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle
. Thus,
\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
is orthogonal to
\lvert\phi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle
, proving Item 2. Finally, from the above calculation, we can easily conclude that
{\mathcal{I}}\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
is orthogonal to
\lvert\phi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle

which imply that
\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle
is orthogonal to
\lvert\phi_{x^{\prime},L^{\prime}_{1},R^{\prime}_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R^{\prime}_{2}}\rangle
, proving Item 3.

Wrap-up.

According to
Lemma

3.4
, it is sufficient to bound the maximum of

\|\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle-\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle\|_{2}

over all
x\in[N],L_{1},L_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t},R_{1},R_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{D}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t}
. From the above calculation, this is equivalently reduced to bounding

\|{\mathcal{I}}\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle-{\mathcal{I}}\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle\|_{2}.

Finally, we use
Lemma

4.29
to bound the number of terms in
Equations

46
and
48
to obtain

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{I}}\lvert\psi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle-{\mathcal{I}}\lvert\phi_{x,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\rangle\|^{2}_{2}=O(t/N).

This concludes the proof of
Lemma

7.1
.
∎

7.2
Closeness of
F_{2}^{L,\dagger}
and
F_{2}^{R,\dagger}

Lemma 7.2

(Image Lemma for
F_{2}^{L}
)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
and any normalized state
\lvert\psi\rangle
on registers
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},\mathsf{S}_{1},\mathsf{T}_{1},\mathsf{S}_{2},\mathsf{T}_{2}
such that
\Pi_{\leq t}\lvert\psi\rangle=\lvert\psi\rangle
and
F_{2}^{L,\dagger}\lvert\psi\rangle=0
, it holds that

\|F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}=O(\sqrt{t/N}).

Proof.

Suppose
\lvert\psi\rangle
can be written as

\lvert\psi\rangle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,b\\
L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}},

where
y\in[N]
,
L_{1},L_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t}
and
R_{1},R_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{D}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t}
; recall that
\mathsf{B}
is the adversary’s auxiliary register, and
b
ranges from some finite set that we do not explicitly specify.

Zero condition.

The premise implies that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=F_{2}^{L,\dagger}\cdot\lvert\psi\rangle_{\mathsf{AB}\mathsf{S}_{1}\mathsf{T}_{1}\mathsf{S}_{2}\mathsf{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle=F_{2}^{L,\dagger}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,b\\
L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
(x,y)\in L_{2}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\setminus\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}.

(by
Equation

4
)

By re-writing
L_{2}=L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,b\\
L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\end{subarray}}\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\},R_{2}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L^{\prime}_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,b\\
L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\end{subarray}}\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\},R_{2}}\right)\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L^{\prime}_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}.

Hence, for any
x\in[N]
,
b
, and
L_{1}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t},L^{\prime}_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t-1},R_{1},R_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{D}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t}
, it holds that

\sum_{y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})}\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\},R_{2}}=0.

(49)

Computing
F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle
.

Next, we will compute
F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle
. Firstly, by
Lemma

4.34
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert\psi\rangle\xmapsto{{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,b,\\
L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+3}}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L_{1}^{(k_{1},k_{3})}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}.

Before we move on to apply
F^{L,\dagger}
, recall
Equation

4
. For the expression to be nonzero, it is necessary that
y\oplus k_{3}\in L^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup L^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}_{2}
. By
Lemma

4.18
,
L^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}
and
L^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}_{2}
are disjoint. Therefore,
(x,y)
must belong to exactly one of the following: (i)
(x,y)\in L^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}
, (ii)
(x,y)\in L^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}_{2}
. Define two projectors

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(L,\mathbf{k})\colon G^{\ell}_{L,k_{2}}\text{ is decomposable}\\
y\in\imaginary(V_{\sf isolate}(G^{\ell}_{L,k_{2}})\cup V_{\sf source}(G^{\ell}_{L,k_{2}}))\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle\!\langle y\rvert_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\lvert L\rangle\!\langle L\rvert_{\mathsf{S}}\otimes\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle\!\langle\mathbf{k}\rvert_{\mathsf{K}},

\displaystyle\Pi_{2}\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(L,\mathbf{k})\colon G^{\ell}_{L,k_{2}}\text{ is decomposable}\\
y\in\imaginary(V_{\sf target}(G^{\ell}_{L,k_{2}}))\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle\!\langle y\rvert_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\lvert L\rangle\!\langle L\rvert_{\mathsf{S}}\otimes\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle\!\langle\mathbf{k}\rvert_{\mathsf{K}}

Thus, it holds that

\displaystyle F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle=F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{1}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle+F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{2}X^{k_{3}}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle.

By the triangle inequality, it suffices to the bound the norm of each term.

Bounding
F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{1}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle
.

Recall
Lemma

4.35
. For any
(y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2})
, define the set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\coloneqq\{(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\colon y\in\imaginary(L^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup L_{2,\sf source}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})})\}.

By respectively sampling
k_{3}
and
\vec{z}_{L}
at the end and the union bound, it is clear that
{\mathcal{P}}_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}
occupies at most a
2t/N
fraction of its universe. Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\|F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{1}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{1}{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}+\|F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{1}({\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet})\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Pi_{1}{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}+\|({\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}-{\mathcal{S}})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq O(\sqrt{t/N}).

The first term is zero by the definitions of
\{{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}
. The second term is bounded by
Lemma

4.35

Bounding
F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{2}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle
.

Using
Equation

4
, we obtain

\displaystyle F^{L,\dagger}\Pi_{2}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,L_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2},\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
(x,y)\in L^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}_{2,\sf target}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+4}}}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup L^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}_{2}\setminus\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}.

By substituting
L_{2}=L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\}
and
x=z_{L,i}\oplus k_{2}
, where
i
is the index such that
y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,L_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}L^{\prime}_{2}},R_{1},R_{2}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}x^{\prime},y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})}\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\}},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}(|L^{\prime}_{2}|+1)}+|R_{2}|+4}}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}z_{L,i}\oplus k_{2}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}

\displaystyle\hskip 68.99895pt\otimes\lvert L^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup({\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\}})^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\setminus\{({\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}z_{L,i}\oplus k_{2}},y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}.

By
Lemma

4.20
, the state is in the domain of the partial isometry
{\mathcal{D}}
. Thus, by
Equation

16
, we can instead calculate the norm of the following state:

\displaystyle\xmapsto{{\mathcal{D}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
x^{\prime},y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\lvert z_{L,i}\oplus k_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}

\displaystyle\hskip 103.50105pt\otimes\lvert L_{1}\cup\{(x^{\prime}\oplus k_{1},z_{L,i}\oplus k_{3})\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L^{\prime}_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}\lvert\vec{z}_{L,-i}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{L}}}\lvert\vec{z}_{R}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{R}}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}.

We now apply the following sequence of partial isometries to simply the expression without changing the norm:

\displaystyle\xmapsto{F^{L}_{\mathsf{extract}}\cdot X^{k_{3}}\cdot X^{k_{2}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
x^{\prime},y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}z_{L,i}\oplus k_{3}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A^{\prime}}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}x^{\prime}\oplus k_{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}L_{1}}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L^{\prime}_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}\lvert\vec{z}_{L,-i}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{L}}}\lvert\vec{z}_{R}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{R}}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}

\displaystyle\xmapsto{X^{k_{3}}\otimes X^{k_{1}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
x^{\prime},y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}z_{L,i}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A^{\prime}}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}x^{\prime}}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L^{\prime}_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}\lvert\vec{z}_{L,-i}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{L}}}\lvert\vec{z}_{R}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{R}}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}.

By substituting
\vec{q}_{L}=\vec{z}_{L,-i}
,
z=z_{i}
, and
\vec{z}_{L}=\vec{q}^{\,(i\leftarrow z)}_{L}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2}\\
x^{\prime},y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}\\
\mathbf{k},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\vec{q}_{L}\in[N]^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|-1}},\,\vec{z}_{R},\,{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}z\in[N]:}\\
(\mathbf{k},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\vec{q}^{\,(i\leftarrow z)}_{L}},\vec{z}_{R})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}z}\rangle_{\mathsf{A^{\prime}}}\lvert x^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert b\rangle_{\mathsf{B}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L^{\prime}_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}\lvert{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\vec{q}_{L}}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{L}}}\lvert\vec{z}_{R}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{R}}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}.

We may compute its squared norm:

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2},x^{\prime}\\
\mathbf{k},\,\vec{q}_{L}\in[N]^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|-1},\,\vec{z}_{R},\,z\in[N]\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}\cdot\Biggl|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2}):\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}\\
(\mathbf{k},\vec{q}^{\,(i\leftarrow z)}_{L},\vec{z}_{R})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}\Biggr|^{2}.

By
Lemma

4.28
, once
(L^{\prime}_{1},L_{2},R_{1},R_{2},x^{\prime},\mathbf{k},\vec{q},\vec{z}_{R},z)
is fixed, there is either zero or at least
N-g(t)
values of
y
that satisfy the condition, where
g(t)=O(t)
is some function guaranteed by
Lemma

4.28
. Let
\mathsf{BAD}
denote the set of tuples for which the latter case holds. We obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{b,{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}(L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2},x^{\prime},\mathbf{k},\vec{q}_{L},\vec{z}_{R},z)\in\mathsf{Bad}}}\frac{1}{N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}\cdot\Biggl|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2}):\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}\\
(\mathbf{k},\vec{q}^{\,(i\leftarrow z)}_{L},\vec{z}_{R})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}\Biggr|^{2}.

Now, we make crucial use of the condition implied by the premise (
Equation

49
) to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{b,(L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2},x^{\prime},\mathbf{k},\vec{q}_{L},\vec{z}_{R},z)\in\mathsf{Bad}}\frac{1}{N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}\cdot\Biggl|{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}-}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2}):\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}\\
(\mathbf{k},\vec{q}^{\,(i\leftarrow z)}_{L},\vec{z}_{R}){\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\notin}\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}\Biggr|^{2}.

Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the definition of
\mathsf{BAD}
to bound the number of
y
in the sum, we can bound it by

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,(L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2},x^{\prime},\mathbf{k},\vec{q}_{L},\vec{z}_{R},z)\in\mathsf{Bad},y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2}):\\
i\ \text{s.t.}\ y\,\in_{i}\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\cup\{y\}\\
(\mathbf{k},\vec{q}^{\,(i\leftarrow z)}_{L},\vec{z}_{R})\notin\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\end{subarray}}\frac{g(t)}{N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}\cdot\Biggl|\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}\Biggr|^{2}.

Since we are summing over non-negative terms, by relaxing the constraints, we can bound it by

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2},x^{\prime},\mathbf{k},\vec{q}_{L},\vec{z}_{R},z),y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\end{subarray}}\frac{g(t)}{N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+5}}\cdot\Biggl|\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}\Biggr|^{2}.

By summing over
(\mathbf{k},\vec{q}_{L},\vec{z}_{R},z)
, and noting that there are at most
N^{|L^{\prime}_{2}|+|R_{2}|+4}
such tuples, we can bounded it by

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b,x^{\prime},L_{1},L^{\prime}_{2},R_{1},R_{2},y\notin\imaginary(L^{\prime}_{2})\end{subarray}}\frac{g(t)}{N}\cdot\Biggl|\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\},R_{2}}\Biggr|^{2}.

By substituting
L=L^{\prime}_{2}\cup\{(x^{\prime},y)\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{g(t)}{N}\cdot\sum_{b,L_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}L_{2}},R_{1},R_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}y\in\imaginary(L_{2})}}\Bigl|\alpha_{y,b,L_{1},R_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}L_{2}},R_{2}}\Bigr|^{2}=O(t/N)

by the normalization condition of
\lvert\psi\rangle
.
∎

Lemma 7.3

(Closeness of
F_{2}^{L,\dagger}
and
F_{2}^{R,\dagger}
)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(\sqrt{t/N})

\displaystyle\|(F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(\sqrt{t/N}).

Proof.

Let
\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}
denote the projection onto the image of
F_{2}^{L}
. For an arbitrary state
\lvert\psi\rangle
in the subspace of
\Pi_{\leq t}
, we can decompose it as

\displaystyle\lvert\psi\rangle=\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle+(\mathrm{id}-\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})})\lvert\psi\rangle.

We will show the following two bounds

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L,\dagger})\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})

(50)

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L,\dagger})(\mathrm{id}-\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})})\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N}).

(51)

which then complete the proof by the triangle inequality. Notice that
F_{2}^{L,\dagger}(\mathrm{id}-\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})})=0
. Thus,
Equation

51
can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L,\dagger})(\mathrm{id}-\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})})\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}(\mathrm{id}-\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})})\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}(\mathrm{id}-\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})})\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

(by
Lemma

3.3
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(\sqrt{t/N}).

(by
Lemma

7.2
)

Hence, it suffices to bound
Equation

50
. Since
\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle
is in the image of
F_{2}^{L}
, there exists some state
\lvert\phi\rangle
such that
\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle=F_{2}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle
. Now. we bound
Equation

50
by the triangle inequality as follows:

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L,\dagger})\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L,\dagger})F_{2}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}-{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}})\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}

(52)

\displaystyle\hskip 68.99895pt+\|({\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L,\dagger}F_{2}^{L})\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}.

(53)

We use
Lemma

7.1
and that operator norm is submultiplicative to bound
Equation

52
. Finally, we use
Lemma

3.10
to bound
Equation

53
by replacing
F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}
and
F_{2}^{L,\dagger}F_{2}^{L}
with the identity. This completes the proof.
∎

We need the following corollaries for the next subsection. The structure of the proof is similar to that of
Lemma

7.3
. We sketch the proof below and omit the details.

Corollary 7.4

(Closeness of
F_{2}^{L}F_{2}^{L,\dagger}
and
F_{2}^{R}F_{2}^{R,\dagger}
)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|(F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}F_{2}^{L,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(\sqrt{t/N})\quad\text{and}\quad\|(F^{R}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R}F_{2}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=O(\sqrt{t/N}).

Proof sketch.

Let
\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle=F_{2}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle
. We will show that

\displaystyle F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle\approx{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}F_{2}^{L,\dagger}\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle.

For the left-hand side, consider the following sequence of hybrids:

\displaystyle F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\phi\rangle

(
{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}\approx F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}
by
Lemma

7.1
)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\phi\rangle.

(
F_{2}^{L,\dagger}F_{2}^{L}\approx\mathrm{id}
by
Lemma

3.10
)

For the right-hand side, consider the following sequence of hybrids:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}F_{2}^{L,\dagger}\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{L})}\lvert\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}F_{2}^{L,\dagger}F_{2}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}\lvert\phi\rangle

(
F_{2}^{L,\dagger}F_{2}^{L}\approx\mathrm{id}
by
Lemma

3.10
)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\phi\rangle.

(
{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}\approx F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}
by
Lemma

7.1
)

This completes the proof.
∎

7.3
Putting Things Together

We use the above lemmas to prove
Lemma

4.38
. We restate
Lemma

4.38
for convenience.

Lemma 7.5

(
Lemma

4.38
, restated)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

•

Forward query:

\|(FX^{k_{2}}F{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(t/\sqrt{N})
,

•

Inverse query:

\|(F^{\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(t/\sqrt{N})
.

Proof of
Lemma

4.38
.

Forward query:.

Recall the definition of
F_{2}
:

\displaystyle F_{2}

\displaystyle=F_{2}^{L}\cdot(\mathrm{id}-F_{2}^{R}\cdot F_{2}^{R,\dagger})+(\mathrm{id}-F_{2}^{L}\cdot F_{2}^{L,\dagger})\cdot F_{2}^{R,\dagger}

\displaystyle=F_{2}^{L}-F_{2}^{L}\cdot F_{2}^{R}\cdot F_{2}^{R,\dagger}+F_{2}^{R,\dagger}-F_{2}^{L}\cdot F_{2}^{L,\dagger}\cdot F_{2}^{R,\dagger}.

(54)

We expand
FX^{k_{2}}F
in the following way:

\displaystyle F\cdot X^{k_{2}}\cdot F

\displaystyle=\Big(F^{L}+\underbrace{(\mathrm{id}-F^{L}\cdot F^{R}-F^{L}\cdot F^{L,\dagger})\cdot F^{R,\dagger}}_{A}\Big)\cdot X^{k_{2}}\cdot\Big(F^{R,\dagger}+\underbrace{F^{L}\cdot(\mathrm{id}-F^{R,\dagger}\cdot F^{R}-F^{L,\dagger}\cdot F^{R,\dagger})}_{B}\Big)

\displaystyle=F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}+F^{L}X^{k_{2}}B+AX^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}+AB.

Here, the term
AB
can be viewed as a negligibly small error. Since there is
F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}
in the middle of
AB
, its operator norm is at most
O(t/\sqrt{N})
by
Lemma

3.12
. Thus, it suffices to show the closeness of the remaining terms. We expand and arrange them in the following way:

\displaystyle F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}+F^{L}X^{k_{2}}B+AX^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}-F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}F^{R}F^{R,\dagger}+F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}-F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}

\displaystyle\hskip 103.50105pt-F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{R,\dagger}+F^{L}(\mathrm{id}-F^{R}F^{R,\dagger})X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}.

In what follows, we will show each term in the second line is negligibly close to a corresponding term in
Equation

54
in operator norm, and both terms in the third line have negligibly small operator norms. Concretely, we have the following claims, which together imply the lemma:

1.

\|(F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})

2.

\|(F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}F^{R}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}F_{2}^{R}F_{2}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})

3.

\|(F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})

4.

\|(F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{R,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}F_{2}^{L,\dagger}F_{2}^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})

5.

\|F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(t/\sqrt{N})

6.

\|F^{L}(\mathrm{id}-F^{R}F^{R,\dagger})X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq O(\sqrt{t/N})

Items 1 and 3 immediately follow from
Lemmas

7.1
and
7.3
, respectively. To prove Item 2, we use the following sequence of hybrids to sketch the proof:

\displaystyle F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}F^{R}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R}F_{2}^{R,\dagger}}

(
F^{L}F^{R}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\approx{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R}F_{2}^{R,\dagger}
by
Corollary

7.4
)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}}F_{2}^{R}F_{2}^{R,\dagger}.

(
F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}{\mathcal{S}}\approx{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{L}
by
Lemma

7.1
)

Item 4 can be proven in a similar way using
Lemma

7.3
and
Corollary

7.4
. To prove Item 5, we use a similar idea as in the proof of
Lemma

7.3
. Suppose
\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{R})}\lvert\psi\rangle=F_{2}^{R}\lvert\phi\rangle
. We can bound it as follows:

\displaystyle\|F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{R})}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{R,\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}F^{R}X^{k_{2}}F^{R}{\mathcal{S}}}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}+O(\sqrt{t/N})

(
{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R}\approx F^{R}X^{k_{2}}F^{R}
by
Lemma

7.1
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|F^{L}X^{k_{2}}F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{R}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}+O(\sqrt{t/N})

(
F^{R,\dagger}F^{R}\approx\mathrm{id}
by
Lemma

3.10
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|F^{L,\dagger}X^{k_{2}}F^{R}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+O(\sqrt{t/N})

(by
Lemma

3.3
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(t/\sqrt{N}).

(by
Lemma

3.12
)

To prove Item 6, we use the same decomposition on
\lvert\psi\rangle
and obtain:

\displaystyle\|F^{L}(\mathrm{id}-F^{R}F^{R,\dagger})X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}\Pi^{\imaginary(F_{2}^{R})}\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|F^{L}(\mathrm{id}-F^{R}F^{R,\dagger})X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|F^{L}(\mathrm{id}-F^{R}F^{R,\dagger})X^{k_{2}}F^{R,\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}F^{R}X^{k_{2}}F^{R}{\mathcal{S}}}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}+O(\sqrt{t/N})

(
{\mathcal{S}}F_{2}^{R}\approx F^{R}X^{k_{2}}F^{R}
by
Lemma

7.1
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|F^{L}(\mathrm{id}-F^{R}F^{R,\dagger})F^{R}{\mathcal{S}}\lvert\phi\rangle\|_{2}+O(\sqrt{t/N})

(
F^{R,\dagger}F^{R}\approx\mathrm{id}
by
Lemma

3.10
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(\sqrt{t/N}).

(
F^{R,\dagger}F^{R}\approx\mathrm{id}
by
Lemma

3.10
)

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
Path-Recording with Independent Left and Right Operators

For the strong path-recording isometry defined in
[
MH25
]
, the left (resp. right) isometry
V^{L}
(resp.
V^{R}
) outputs strings
y
(resp.
x
) that are in in the image (resp. domain) of
both
the left and right relation states in the purifying register. In our case, however, it will be easier to work with a similar pair of isometries that only look at their own relation state, as opposed to both relations taken together. In this section we define a pair of new isometries,
F^{L}
and
F^{R}
that are independent of each other.

A.1
Closeness to the Path-Recording Isometries

Lemma A.1
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|(V^{L}-F^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{t(t+2)}{N}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|(V^{R}-F^{R})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{t(t+2)}{N}}.

Proof.

Consider an arbitrary (normalized) state in the support of
\Pi_{\leq t}

\lvert\psi\rangle_{\mathsf{AST}}=\sum_{x,L,R}\alpha_{x,L,R}\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}},

where
\alpha_{x,L,R}=0
whenever
|L|
or
|R|>t
. Then we expand out

\displaystyle V^{L}\lvert\psi\rangle_{\mathsf{AST}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,L,R}\frac{\alpha_{x,L,R}}{\sqrt{N-|\imaginary(L\cup R)|}}\sum_{y\notin\imaginary(L\cup R)}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}},\;\text{and}

\displaystyle F^{L}\lvert\psi\rangle_{\mathsf{AST}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,L,R}\frac{\alpha_{x,L,R}}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{y\notin\imaginary(L)}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}.

Subtracting,

\displaystyle(V^{L}

\displaystyle-F^{L})\lvert\psi\rangle_{\mathsf{AST}}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{x,L,R}\alpha_{x,L,R}\sum_{y\notin\imaginary(L\cup R)}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-|\imaginary(L\cup R)|}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)}_{\lvert v\rangle}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{x,L,R}\alpha_{x,L,R}\sum_{y\in\imaginary(R)\setminus\imaginary(L)}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L\cup\{(x,y)\}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\left(-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)}_{\lvert w\rangle}.

Note that
\lvert w\rangle
and
\lvert v\rangle
are orthogonal, since
\lvert v\rangle
is a superposition of states
\lvert y\rangle\lvert L^{\prime}\rangle\lvert R\rangle
where
y
appears exactly once in
\imaginary(L^{\prime})
and does not appear in
\imaginary(R)
, while
\lvert w\rangle
is a superposition of states
\lvert y\rangle\lvert L^{\prime}\rangle\lvert R\rangle
where
y
appears exactly once in both
\imaginary(L^{\prime})
and
\imaginary(R)
. Thus,

\norm{(V^L - F^L)\ket{\psi}}^{2}_{2}=\langle v|v\rangle+\langle w|w\rangle.

Bounding
\langle v|v\rangle
.
Similar to
[
MH25
]
, by changing the order of summation, we can rewrite
\lvert v\rangle
as

\displaystyle\lvert v\rangle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,L^{\prime},R\end{subarray}}\lvert y\rangle\lvert L^{\prime}\rangle\lvert R\rangle\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{x,L,R}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)\right),

and thus

\displaystyle\langle v|v\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,L^{\prime},R\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{x,L,R}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,L^{\prime},R\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\absolutevalue{\alpha_{x,L,R}}^{2}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)^{2}\right),

where the last inequality is by Cauchy-Schwarz. We can bound the summand by writing

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{N}-\sqrt{N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}}{\sqrt{N(N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)})}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}}{\sqrt{N(N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)})}}\right)^{2}

(since
\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}\leq\sqrt{a-b}
when
a\geq b\geq 0
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\frac{\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}{N(N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\absolutevalue{L}+1)\cdot\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}{N(N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)})}

where the last inequality uses the fact that for any fixed
L^{\prime}
, there are at most
\absolutevalue{L}+1
choices of
(x,L)
that can satisfy
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\langle v|v\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\absolutevalue{L}+1)\cdot\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}{N(N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)})}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,L^{\prime},R\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\},\\
y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\end{subarray}}\absolutevalue{\alpha_{x,L,R}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(\absolutevalue{L}+1)\cdot\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}{N(N-\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)})}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,L,R\end{subarray}}\absolutevalue{\alpha_{x,L,R}}^{2}\cdot\left(\sum_{y\in[N]}\mathds{1}(y\not\in\imaginary(L\cup R))\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\absolutevalue{L}+1)\cdot\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}{N}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,L,R\end{subarray}}\absolutevalue{\alpha_{x,L,R}}^{2}=\frac{(\absolutevalue{L}+1)\cdot\absolutevalue{\Im(L \cup R)}}{N}.

Bounding
\langle w|w\rangle
.
We know that

\lvert w\rangle=\frac{-1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{y,(L^{\prime},R)}\lvert y\rangle\lvert L^{\prime}\rangle\lvert R\rangle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
y\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\setminus\imaginary(L)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{x,L,R}

Then

\displaystyle\langle w|w\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{y,(L^{\prime},R)}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
y\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\setminus\imaginary(L)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{x,L,R}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{y,(L^{\prime},R)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,L):\\
L^{\prime}=L\cup\{(x,y)\}\\
y\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\setminus\imaginary(L)\end{subarray}}\left|\alpha_{x,L,R}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{x,L,R}\left|\alpha_{x,L,R}\right|^{2}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in\imaginary(L\cup R)\setminus\imaginary(L)\end{subarray}}1\right)\leq\frac{t}{N}\sum_{x,L,R}\left|\alpha_{x,L,R}\right|^{2}=\frac{t}{N}

Hence, it holds that

\|(V^{L}-F^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{t(t+2)}{N}}.

By a symmetric argument, we have

\|(V^{R}-F^{R})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{t(t+2)}{N}}.\qed

We have the following corollaries.

Corollary A.2
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|(V^{L,\dagger}-F^{L,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{t(t+2)}{N}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|(V^{R,\dagger}-F^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{t(t+2)}{N}}.

Proof.

Using the fact that
\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|A^{\dagger}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
for any operator
A
, we have

\displaystyle\|(V^{L,\dagger}-F^{L,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|\Pi_{\leq t}(V^{L}-F^{L})\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Next, since applying
V^{L}
and
F^{L}
only increase the size of each relation on the register
\mathsf{S}
by one, we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{\leq t}(V^{L}-F^{L})=(V^{L}-F^{L})\Pi_{\leq t-1}.

Finally, the desired bound then follows from
Lemma

A.1
. The second bound follows by the same argument.
∎

Finally, we have:

Lemma A.3
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|(V-F)\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 8\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(t+2)(t+4)}{N}}.

Proof.

Recall the definitions

V=V^{L}\cdot(\mathrm{id}-V^{R}\cdot V^{R,\dagger})+(\mathrm{id}-V^{L}\cdot V^{L,\dagger})\cdot V^{R,\dagger}\quad\text{and}\quad F=F^{L}\cdot(\mathrm{id}-F^{R}\cdot F^{R,\dagger})+(\mathrm{id}-F^{L}\cdot F^{L,\dagger})\cdot F^{R,\dagger}.

By the triangle inequality and the above expression, we have

\displaystyle\norm{(V - F) \Pi_{\leq t}}_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\norm{(V^L - F^L) \Pi_{\leq t}}_{\mathrm{op}}+\norm{(V^L V^R V^{R,\dagger} - F^L F^R F^{R,\dagger}) \Pi_{\leq t}}_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\norm{(V^L V^{L,\dagger} V^{R,\dagger} - F^L F^{L,\dagger} F^{R,\dagger}) \Pi_{\leq t}}_{\mathrm{op}}+\norm{(V^{R,\dagger} - F^{R,\dagger}) \Pi_{\leq t}}_{\mathrm{op}}.

We will bound each term in the rest of the proof. From
Lemma

A.1
and
Corollary

A.2
, we can bound the first term and the last term by

\|(V^{L}-F^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{t(t+2)}{N}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|(V^{R,\dagger}-F^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{t(t+2)}{N}}.

Next, by the triangle inequality, we can bound the second term by

\displaystyle\|(V^{L}V^{R}V^{R,\dagger}-F^{L}F^{R}F^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(V^{L}V^{R}V^{R,\dagger}-F^{L}V^{R}V^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|(F^{L}V^{R}V^{R,\dagger}-F^{L}F^{R}V^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|(F^{L}F^{R}V^{R,\dagger}-F^{L}F^{R}F^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=\|(V^{L}-F^{L})V^{R}V^{R,\dagger}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|F^{L}(V^{R}-F^{R})F^{R}V^{R,\dagger}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}+\|F^{L}F^{R}(V^{R,\dagger}-F^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

Since applying
V^{R}
and
F^{R}
can increase the size of the relation by at most one, while applying
V^{\dagger}_{R}
and
F^{\dagger}_{R}
never increases it, we can bound the above sum as follows:

\displaystyle\leq\|(V^{L}-F^{L})\Pi_{\leq t+1}V^{R}V^{R,\dagger}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\hskip 68.99895pt+\|F^{L}\Pi_{\leq t+2}(V^{R}-F^{R})\Pi_{\leq t+1}F^{R}\Pi_{\leq t}V^{R,\dagger}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\hskip 137.9979pt+\|F^{L}\Pi_{\leq t+2}F^{R}\Pi_{\leq t+1}(V^{R,\dagger}-F^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(V^{L}-F^{L})\Pi_{\leq t+1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\hskip 68.99895pt+\|F^{L}\Pi_{\leq t+2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\|(V^{R}-F^{R})\Pi_{\leq t+1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\|F^{L}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\hskip 137.9979pt+\|F^{L}\Pi_{\leq t+2}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\|F^{R}\Pi_{\leq t+1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\|(V^{R,\dagger}-F^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

(by submultiplicity of operator norm)

\displaystyle\leq 3\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(t+2)(t+4)}{N}}.

(by
Lemma

A.1
,
Corollary

A.2
and the fact that
F^{L}
and
F^{R}
are contractions)

Similarly, we can bound the third term by

\|(V^{L}V^{L,\dagger}V^{R,\dagger}-F^{L}F^{L,\dagger}F^{R,\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(t+2)(t+4)}{N}}.

Collecting the bounds, we have

\|(V-F)\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 8\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(t+2)(t+4)}{N}}

as desired.
∎

Using a symmetric argument, we also have:

Lemma A.4
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\|(V^{\dagger}-F^{\dagger})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 8\cdot\sqrt{\frac{(t+2)(t+4)}{N}}.

Lemma A.5
.

For any adversary
{\cal A}
that makes
t
forward queries and
t
inverse queries,

\norm{\ket{\Adversary^{F, F^{\dagger}}}_{\reg{ABST}}
- \ket{\Adversary^{V, V^{\dagger}}}_{\reg{ABST}}}_{2}=O\quantity(\sqrt{\frac{t^4}{N}}).

Proof.

The lemma follows from
Lemmas

A.3
and
A.4
and a sequence of hybrids that replace query to
F
with
V
one-by-one.
∎

A.2
Missing Proofs in
Section

3.4

Lemma A.6

(
Lemma

3.10
, restated)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}-\mathrm{id})\Pi_{\leq t}\|\leq t/N\quad\text{and}\quad\|(F^{R,\dagger}F^{R}-\mathrm{id})\Pi_{\leq t}\|\leq t/N

Proof.

For any
x,y\in[N],L\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}},R\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{D}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}
such that
|L|+|R|\leq t
, from
Equations

4
and
5
, we have

\displaystyle F^{L,\dagger}\cdot F^{L}\cdot\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}=\frac{N-|L|}{N}\cdot\lvert x\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\quad\text{and}\quad F^{R,\dagger}\cdot F^{R}\cdot\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}=\frac{N-|R|}{N}\cdot\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert R\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}.

Therefore, by
Lemma

3.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|(F^{L,\dagger}F^{L}-\mathrm{id})\Pi_{\leq t}\|=\max_{x,L\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}_{\leq t}}\frac{|L|}{N}=\frac{t}{N}.

The second bound follows from the same argument.
∎

We require the following fact, which is a consequence of
[
MH25
, Definition 37, Claim 22, Equations (11.22) and (11.26)]
.

Fact A.7
.

There exist operators
E^{L}
and
E^{R}
that satisfy

•

For any
t\geq 0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|(V^{L}-E^{L})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{t(t+2)/N}\quad\text{and}\quad\|(V^{R}-E^{R})\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{t(t+2)/N}.

•

For any
\ell,r\geq 0
such that
\ell+r\leq N
, it holds that

\displaystyle E^{L}_{\ell,r}\cdot E^{L,\dagger}_{\ell,r}=\sum_{i\in[\ell+1]}\bigg(\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\cdot\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\cdot\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\bigg)\otimes\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle E^{R}_{\ell,r}\cdot E^{R,\dagger}_{\ell,r}=\sum_{i\in[r+1]}\Pi_{\ell,\mathsf{S}}\otimes\bigg(\Pi_{r+1,\mathsf{T}}\cdot\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{T}^{(r+1)}_{\mathsf{X},i}}\cdot\Pi_{r+1,\mathsf{T}}\bigg).

The operators
E^{L}
and
E^{R}
satisfy the following property.

Lemma A.8
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
and any unitary
U
acting non-trivially on the register
\mathsf{A}
,

\displaystyle\|E^{L,\dagger}UE^{R}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{t(t+1)}/N\quad\text{and}\quad\|E^{R,\dagger}UE^{L}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{t(t+1)}/N\,.

Proof.

By
Lemma

3.4
, we have

\displaystyle\|E^{R,\dagger}UE^{L}\Pi_{\leq t}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{op}}=\max_{0\leq\ell,r\leq t}\|E^{R,\dagger}_{\ell+1,r-1}UE^{L}_{\ell,r}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{op}}.

(55)

We next bound the squared operator norm in the following. Using the fact
\|A\|^{2}_{\mathrm{op}}=\|AA^{\dagger}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|A^{\dagger}A\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:EL_ER_1}=

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq\ell,r\leq t}\|E^{R,\dagger}_{\ell+1,r-1}UE^{L}_{\ell,r}\cdot E^{L,\dagger}_{\ell,r}U^{\dagger}E^{R}_{\ell+1,r}\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq\ell,r\leq t}\sum_{i\in[\ell+1]}\bigg\|E^{R,\dagger}_{\ell+1,r-1}\cdot U\cdot\bigg(\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\cdot\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\otimes\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}}\cdot\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\bigg)\cdot U^{\dagger}\cdot E^{R}_{\ell+1,r-1}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

(56)

Since
U
only acts non-trivially on the register
\mathsf{A}
, we simplify the notation as follows:

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:EL_ER_2}=\max_{0\leq\ell,r\leq t}\sum_{i\in[\ell+1]}\bigg\|E^{R,\dagger}_{\ell+1,r-1}\cdot U_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\cdot\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\otimes\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}}\cdot U_{\mathsf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\cdot E^{R}_{\ell+1,r-1}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

(57)

Using the facts that
\|AA^{\dagger}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|A^{\dagger}A\|_{\mathrm{op}}
and
\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\otimes\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}}
is a projector, we have

(
57
)

\displaystyle=\max_{0\leq\ell,r\leq t}\sum_{i\in[\ell+1]}\bigg\|\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\otimes\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}}\cdot U_{\mathsf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\cdot E^{R}_{\ell+1,r-1}\cdot E^{R,\dagger}_{\ell+1,r-1}\cdot U_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\cdot\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\otimes\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{0\leq\ell,r\leq t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[\ell+1]\\
j\in[r]\end{subarray}}\bigg\|\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\otimes\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}}\cdot U_{\mathsf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\cdot\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{T}^{(r)}_{\mathsf{X},j}}\cdot U_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{S}}\cdot\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\otimes\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}}\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}

(58)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:EL_ER_4}=

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq\ell,r\leq t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[\ell+1]\\
j\in[r]\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\bigg\|\Pi^{\sf EPR}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\otimes\bigg(\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}}\cdot U_{\mathsf{T}^{(r)}_{\mathsf{X},j}}^{\dagger}\cdot\Pi_{\ell+1,\mathsf{T}^{(r)}_{\mathsf{X},j},\mathsf{S}\setminus\mathsf{S}^{(\ell+1)}_{\mathsf{Y},i}}\cdot U_{\mathsf{T}^{(r)}_{\mathsf{X},j}}\cdot\Pi_{r,\mathsf{T}}\bigg)\bigg\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq\ell,r\leq t}\frac{(\ell+1)r}{N^{2}}=\frac{(t+1)t}{N^{2}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.9

(
Lemma

3.12
, restated)
.

For any integer
t\geq 0
and any unitary
U
acting non-trivially on the register
\mathsf{A}
,

\displaystyle\|F^{L,\dagger}UF^{R}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\sqrt{t(t+2)/N}\quad\text{and}\quad\|F^{R,\dagger}UF^{L}\Pi_{\leq t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 3\sqrt{t(t+2)/N}\,.

Proof.

It immediately follows from
Lemmas

A.1
,
A.7
and
A.8

together with the triangle inequality.
∎

Lemma A.10

(
Lemma

4.35
, restated)
.

Let
\{{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\}_{\tau}
be collection of sets where the index
\tau
ranges over
(y\in[N],L_{1}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}},R_{1}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{D}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}},L_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}},R_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{D}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}})
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}\subseteq[N]^{3}\times[N]^{|L_{2}|}\times[N]^{|R_{2}|}
. Define the operator

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle\colon\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+3}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\colon\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\notin{\mathcal{P}}_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\end{subarray}}\lvert L^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert k\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}.

If there exists
\delta\geq 0
such that for any
\tau
,

\displaystyle\frac{\big|{\mathcal{P}}_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\cap\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\big|}{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+3}}\leq\delta,

then

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}-{\mathcal{S}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\sqrt{\delta}.

Proof.

For any normalized state
\lvert\psi\rangle=\sum_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\alpha_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|({\mathcal{S}}^{\bullet}-{\mathcal{S}})\lvert\psi\rangle\|_{2}=\bigg\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\colon\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in{\mathcal{P}}_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+3}}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup L_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{L})}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}}\lvert R^{(k_{1},k_{3})}_{1}\cup R_{2}^{(k_{2},\vec{z}_{R})}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}\bigg\|_{2}.

By
Lemma

4.20
, we can instead apply
{\mathcal{D}}
to the state and bound its squared norm as follows:

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\colon\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in{\mathcal{P}}_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\alpha_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+3}}}\lvert y\rangle_{\mathsf{A}}\lvert L_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{1}}\lvert R_{1}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{1}}\lvert L_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{S}_{2}}\lvert R_{2}\rangle_{\mathsf{T}_{2}}\lvert\vec{z}_{L}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{L}}}\lvert\vec{z}_{R}\rangle_{\mathsf{Z_{R}}}\lvert\mathbf{k}\rangle_{\mathsf{K}}\bigg\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\colon\\
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{z})\in{\mathcal{P}}_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\end{subarray}}\bigg|\frac{\alpha_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}}{\sqrt{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+3}}}\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}|\alpha_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}|^{2}\cdot\frac{\big|{\mathcal{P}}_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\cap\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\big|}{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+3}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\frac{\big|{\mathcal{P}}_{y,L_{1},R_{1},L_{2},R_{2}}\cap\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)\big|}{N^{|L_{2}|+|R_{2}|+3}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta.

∎

Missing Proofs in

Proof of

Lemma B.1

(
Lemma

4.20
, restated)
.

For any
L_{1},L_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{I}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}
,
R_{1},R_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{{\mathcal{D}}\text{-}\mathrm{dist}}
, every tuple in
\mathsf{G}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
R_{1},R_{2}\end{subarray}\right)
satisfies all conditions in
Lemma

4.18
and is therefore robustly decodable.

Proof of
Lemma

4.20
.

Recall
Definition

4.19
. For convenience, we color the conditions according to the properties they enforce in the following definition:
distinctness
,
disjointness
, and
no extra
k_{2}
-correlated pairs
.

1.

k_{1}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\Big(\operatorname{Dom}(L_{1})\oplus\operatorname{Dom}(L_{2})\Big)}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\Big(\operatorname{Dom}(R_{1})\oplus\operatorname{Dom}(R_{2})\Big)}

2.

k_{2}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.0924,0.44,0.0924}\Bigg(\bigg(\Big(\operatorname{Dom}(L_{1})\oplus k_{1}\Big)\cup\operatorname{Dom}(L_{2})\bigg)\oplus\bigg(\Big(\imaginary(L_{1})\oplus k_{3}\Big)\cup\imaginary(L_{2})\bigg)\Bigg)}\\
\cup{\color[rgb]{0.0924,0.44,0.0924}\Bigg(\bigg(\Big(\operatorname{Dom}(R_{1})\oplus k_{1}\Big)\cup\operatorname{Dom}(R_{2})\bigg)\oplus\bigg(\Big(\imaginary(R_{1})\oplus k_{3}\Big)\cup\imaginary(R_{2})\bigg)\Bigg)}

3.

k_{3}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\Big(\imaginary(L_{1})\oplus\imaginary(L_{2})\Big)}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\Big(\imaginary(R_{1})\oplus\imaginary(R_{2})\Big)}

4.

\vec{z}_{L}\in{\color[rgb]{0.2975,0.2975,0.85}[N]^{|L_{2}|}_{\mathrm{dist}}}
and
\vec{z}_{R}\in{\color[rgb]{0.2975,0.2975,0.85}[N]^{|R_{2}|}_{\mathrm{dist}}}

5.

\{\vec{z}_{L}\}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\Big(\imaginary(L_{1})\oplus k_{3}\Big)}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.2975,0.2975,0.85}\imaginary(L_{2})}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.0924,0.44,0.0924}\Bigg(\bigg(\Big(\operatorname{Dom}(L_{1})\oplus k_{1}\Big)\cup\operatorname{Dom}(L_{2})\bigg)\oplus k_{2}\Bigg)}
are disjoint.

6.

\{\vec{z}_{R}\}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.72,0.1512,0.1512}\Big(\imaginary(R_{1})\oplus k_{3}\Big)}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.2975,0.2975,0.85}\imaginary(R_{2})}\cup{\color[rgb]{0.0924,0.44,0.0924}\Bigg(\bigg(\Big(\operatorname{Dom}(R_{1})\oplus k_{1}\Big)\cup\operatorname{Dom}(R_{2})\bigg)\oplus k_{2}\Bigg)}
are disjoint.

Each condition can be verified directly.
∎
